- **Last session summary (2026-11-11):**
  - The owner asked what else should be tested. I proposed four additions: cross-module integration tests, a 32-bit build, ARM struct layout checks, and fuzzing. The owner chose the 32-bit build (P18).
  - A trial `-m32` build found **bug 225**: `SerialFFB` checks the `int64_t` command address against `size_t`. That comparison is signed on the 32-bit target, so a negative address reads or writes outside the effects array. Everything else behaved identically on 32-bit.
  - T1 added the `FFB_TESTS_32BIT` option. T2 added 31 cases (`test_serial_ffb_32bit.cpp`), the markers `KNOWN_ISSUE_32BIT` / `KNOWN_ISSUE_32BIT_UB`, and `run_all_tests.py` support (one writer, one reviewer, 42 mutants). T3's warning diff over 586 compile commands found no further width-dependent behaviour.

## Decisions log

| Date | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| 2026-11-04 | **P11: CMSIS-DSP in scope.** My choices: scope = all functions and data types of the modules the firmware uses (plus their helpers); oracle = ARM reference patterns (`Testing/Patterns`) + math definition; phase P11 with a stop after each module group | My request |
| 2026-10-20 | Extend the existing doctest suite in `Firmware/Tests/` instead of creating a new `Tests/` suite | It already exists with its CMake setup. A second suite would duplicate it and drift |
| 2026-10-20 | Rule is "`Firmware/` read-only except `Firmware/Tests/`" | Existing suite lives inside `Firmware/` |
| 2026-10-20 | Build out-of-tree | Nothing should be written under `Firmware/`, not even gitignored build files |
| 2026-10-20 | Use default Makefiles generator | Ninja is only a shell alias, not on PATH |
| 2026-10-20 | Exclude the `effectsForces` case, do not fix firmware, do not disable `_GLIBCXX_ASSERTIONS` globally | It is a real suspected bug. Assertions may catch others |
| 2026-10-20 | Exact exclusion name: `requesting the forces of a not existing axis is rejected` (`cases/test_effects_calculator.cpp:1062`, suite `EffectsCalculator`) | Confirmed from the source and from the abort output |
| 2026-10-20 | Build root `../ffb-test-build` (sibling of the repo checkout), one subdirectory per variant | Outside the repo and stable across sessions |
| 2026-10-21 | Q1: driver table confirmed. `TMCDebugBridge`, `MidiMain` and `MidiHandler` are out of scope. MIDI removed from the plan, MIDI feature macro stays off | My decision |
| 2026-10-21 | Q2: vendor code is tested in tiers. A: `eeprom.c`, FreeRTOS C++ wrappers, both Python helpers. B: TinyUSB device code vs a fake USB controller. C: only the CMSIS-DSP functions the firmware calls. Not tested: ST HAL, CMSIS core, FreeRTOS kernel, `Targets/**`, CubeMX code. Vendor bugs are reported, never fixed | My decision |
| 2026-10-21 | Q3: keep ASan on the whole build (including CMSIS-DSP). In sanitizer runs skip only the 2 spline cases. Keep bug #2 | Vendor DSP code is now in scope (Tier C) |
| 2026-10-21 | Q4/Q5: do F1. `ffb_unit_tests` stays exactly as is (same sources, same feature set, same fake `Axis.h`). New executables get their own feature header (real F407 `target_constants.h` minus driver macros, MIDI off; show me the diff before use). Firmware sources compiled per executable, only platform fakes shared. After every CMake change `ffb_unit_tests` must still give 316 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped | Keeps the existing suite green and CI-safe |

## Environment

All commands run from the repo root (`OpenFFBoard/`). They are plain POSIX commands (work in bash/zsh; in fish replace `$(nproc)` with `(nproc)`).

- Host OS / compiler / generator: Linux (CachyOS, kernel 7.2), GCC 16.2.1, clang 23.1.3, CMake 4.4.4, Unix Makefiles (Ninja not on PATH)
- Test framework: doctest 2.4.11 (`Firmware/Tests/Code_Coverage/third_party/doctest.h`, do not edit)
- Test source location: `Firmware/Tests/` (documented in `Firmware/Tests/README.md`; README requires CMake >= 3.16, C++20, MinGW-w64 on Windows)
- CMSIS-DSP submodule: checked out, so the default build has `USE_DSP_FUNCTIONS=ON` (`FFB_TESTS_USE_DSP=AUTO`)
- Build directory (out-of-tree): `../ffb-test-build/<variant>` (variants: `default`, `nodsp`, `san`, `nodsp-san`, `clang23`, `clang23-san`, `cov`, `m32`, `preset-host-sanitize`). `../ffb-test-build/clang` is a broken leftover (configure failed); delete it by hand
- Configure: `cmake -S Firmware/Tests -B ../ffb-test-build/default`
  - no DSP: add `-DFFB_TESTS_USE_DSP=OFF`
  - ASan+UBSan: add `-DFFB_TESTS_SANITIZE=ON`
  - 32-bit (P18): add `-DFFB_TESTS_32BIT=ON`. This needs gcc or clang with 32-bit multilib on Linux; configure stops with a message otherwise (MSVC, macOS). It builds every executable with `-m32 -msse2 -mfpmath=sse -funsigned-char`. Its effects:
    - extra ctest exclusion `FFB_TARGET_TESTS_EXCLUDE_32BIT` (2 cases, bug 225);
    - the 5 `__int128` DSP test files are left out;
    - `KNOWN_ISSUE_32BIT(_UB)` cases fail as expected or are skipped.
  - Real ST HAL (P12): add `-DFFB_TESTS_32BIT=ON -DFFB_TESTS_REAL_HAL=ON` (Linux only; configure stops otherwise). Adds `ffb_hal_tests`, `ffb_hal_tests_disco` and `hal_identity_f407vg_disco` to ctest (14 tests). Gate build dir used: `../ffb-test-build/p12t0-32hal`.

    Verified on this host (GCC 16).
  - clang: add `-DCMAKE_C_COMPILER=clang -DCMAKE_CXX_COMPILER=clang++`
  - coverage: add `-DCMAKE_C_FLAGS=--coverage -DCMAKE_CXX_FLAGS=--coverage -DCMAKE_EXE_LINKER_FLAGS=--coverage`
- Build: `cmake --build ../ffb-test-build/default -j"$(nproc)"`
- Run (with exclusion): `../ffb-test-build/default/ffb_unit_tests --test-case-exclude="requesting the forces of a not existing axis is rejected"` (`-tce=` is the short form)
- Sanitizer run: `../ffb-test-build/san/ffb_unit_tests --test-case-exclude="requesting the forces of a not existing axis is rejected,reconstruction of a constant signal is constant,reconstruction of a step never leaves the range of the input values and reaches the target"`
- `ctest`: `ctest --test-dir ../ffb-test-build/default --output-on-failure`. Runs 8 tests (`ffb_driver_tests` (P10 driver track, `FFB_DRIVER_TESTS_EXCLUDE`, `FFB_DRIVER_TESTS_EXCLUDE_SANITIZE`), `ffb_flash_tests` (P3), `ffb_rtos_tests` (P8, exclusion lists `FFB_RTOS_TESTS_EXCLUDE`, `FFB_RTOS_TESTS_EXCLUDE_SANITIZE`), `ffb_usb_tests` (P9, `FFB_USB_TESTS_EXCLUDE`, `FFB_USB_TESTS_EXCLUDE_SANITIZE`) and `python_helper_tests` (P9, only when Python 3 is found) besides the 3 below): `ffb_unit_tests` with the exclusions (F1/F1b, cache lists `FFB_TESTS_EXCLUDE` and `FFB_TESTS_EXCLUDE_SANITIZE`, printed at configure time: `OpenFFBoard tests: excluded from ctest: ...`), `ffb_unit_tests_baseline` (the baseline check) and `ffb_target_tests` (P2, cases in `claude/cases_target/`, own exclusion lists `FFB_TARGET_TESTS_EXCLUDE` and (sanitizer builds only) `FFB_TARGET_TESTS_EXCLUDE_SANITIZE`, both default empty, and (32-bit builds only, P18) `FFB_TARGET_TESTS_EXCLUDE_32BIT` with the 2 bug 225 cases, printed as `OpenFFBoard tests: ffb_target_tests excluded from ctest: ...`)
- Run `ffb_target_tests` directly: `../ffb-test-build/default/ffb_target_tests` (no exclusion needed; in sanitizer builds the cases marked `DELETES_FFBHIDMAIN` skip themselves)
- Baseline check (gate step 2): `python3 -B Firmware/Tests/Code_Coverage/tools/check_baseline.py --build-dir ../ffb-test-build/<variant>` (reads exclusions and sanitizer flag from `CMakeCache.txt`; exit 0 ok, 1 changed/missing, 2 crash). `--print-current` prints the current classification
- Presets: `CMakePresets.json` puts build dirs inside `Firmware/Tests/`. To use a preset out-of-tree: `cd Firmware/Tests && cmake --preset host-sanitize -B ../../../ffb-test-build/preset-host-sanitize`, then `cmake --build ../../../ffb-test-build/preset-host-sanitize -j"$(nproc)"`, then `ctest --test-dir <that dir>` (not `ctest --preset`, which uses the in-tree dir)
- Shell note: the interactive shell is fish, the tool shell is zsh; for loops over option strings use `bash -c` (zsh does not split `$var`)
- Coverage: `gcov` only (gcovr/lcov are not installed and must not be installed). Configure `cov` (see above), build, delete old data (`find ../ffb-test-build/cov -name '*.gcda' -delete`), `ctest --test-dir ../ffb-test-build/cov`, then `python3 -B Firmware/Tests/Code_Coverage/tools/coverage_report.py --build-dir ../ffb-test-build/cov` (Markdown to stdout; hardware-only ranges from `tools/coverage_exclusions.txt` are left out, `--no-exclusions` for raw numbers; per file, per tier, ladder total, not-compiled files with estimates; `--headers` adds headers; `--gcov "llvm-cov gcov"` for clang)
- CI (GitHub Actions, read-only, P0): `.github/workflows/build-firmware.yml` (push/PR to `master`/`development`, manual, `workflow_call`: ARM `make` for `F411RE`, `F407VG`, `F407VG_DISCO` via `.github/actions/build-firmware`, uploads firmware), `doxygen.yml` (push to `master`), `release.yml` (tags `v*`: firmware + configurator builds on Windows/macOS, release). **No workflow builds or runs `Firmware/Tests/`**, although the README says the tests run in GitHub Actions. See open question 5
- Presets (`Firmware/Tests/CMakePresets.json`): `host`, `host-mingw` (MinGW Makefiles), `host-nodsp`, `host-sanitize`; configure/build/test presets for each. `host-sanitize` verified in P0 (out-of-tree): ctest 2/2 passed at the time; re-verified in the final review FR1 (2026-10-27): `host`, `host-nodsp`, `host-sanitize` out-of-tree, ctest 5/5 each. `host-mingw` not verifiable here (no Windows)
- Sanity check after any build: `git status --short --ignored Firmware` must show nothing outside `Firmware/Tests/`.
- Full report (nothing skipped): `python3 -B Firmware/Tests/Code_Coverage/tools/run_all_tests.py --build-dir ../ffb-test-build/default` or `cmake --build ../ffb-test-build/default --target test_report`. Runs every case of every executable plus the Python tests; crashing and skip-decorated cases each run in their own process. Writes `<build-dir>/test_report.log` with the summary (Passed / Failed / Bugged), then the failed tests, the bugged tests (known bugs) and the open questions, each with the test location, the file under test and the bug / NEEDS REVIEW number. A failed test is reported and the run carries on; the `test_report` target always succeeds (`--exit-zero`), the script alone exits 1 when Failed > 0. Not part of ctest.
- Summarise a log (driver track, agents read this instead of raw logs): `python3 -B Firmware/Tests/Code_Coverage/tools/summarise_results.py <log or doctest XML>` (counts, failures with the first failed assertion, skipped, excluded, build errors).
- Bug report: `python3 -B Firmware/Tests/Code_Coverage/tools/make_bug_report.py` regenerates `Tests/Code_Coverage/claude/reports/BUG_REPORT.md` from the "Suspected firmware bugs" and "NEEDS REVIEW items" tables (warns about rows with an unescaped `|`). Run it at the end of every phase, before the approval commit.

## Baseline (existing suite, before any new work)

Commit `a5418ff5`. 318 test cases in total (`--list-test-cases`): 317 runnable + 1 already `skip()`-decorated. 17 cases carry a `KNOWN_ISSUE` marker (15 `KNOWN_ISSUE`, 2 `KNOWN_ISSUE_UB`); outside sanitizer builds they are `should_fail` and count as passed while they fail. With the exclusion, 16 of them run ("Failed as expected").

| Variant | Build | Run (with `effectsForces` exclusion) | Runtime |
|---|---|---|---|
| GCC 16, DSP ON, -O0 (default) | OK, 74 warnings (72 `-Wnon-c-typedef-for-linkage`, 1 `-Wmaybe-uninitialized` `Filters.cpp:30`, 1 `-Wformat` `CommandHandler.cpp:136`) | **316 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped**; 2902 assertions, 34 failed inside `should_fail` cases; exit 0 | 0.33 s |
| Same, **unfiltered** | OK | Aborts with SIGABRT in `requesting the forces of a not existing axis is rejected`: 192 cases run, 126 not run, exit 134 | 0.36 s |
| GCC 16, DSP OFF | OK, 73 warnings | 316 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped; exit 0 | not timed |
| clang 23, DSP ON | OK, 483 warnings (mostly `-Winconsistent-missing-override`, `-Wdeprecated-volatile`, `-Wunknown-warning-option`; notable: `-Warray-bounds` `CanCommandInterface.cpp:123`, `-Wformat` `CommandHandler.cpp:136`, `-Wvla-cxx-extension` `CmdParser.cpp:93`, `-Wunused-private-field` `UART.h:69`) | 316 passed, 0 failed, 2 skipped; exit 0 | not timed |
| GCC 16 ASan+UBSan, DSP ON | OK | **ASan stack-buffer-overflow** in CMSIS-DSP `arm_spline_f32` (bug #2), exit 1. With the 2 spline cases also excluded: 313 passed, 0 failed, 5 skipped, no UBSan/LSan findings | not timed |
| clang ASan+UBSan, DSP ON | OK | Same ASan finding; same clean result with the 2 extra exclusions | not timed |
| GCC 16 ASan+UBSan, DSP OFF | OK | 315 passed, 0 failed, 3 skipped, no findings (spline path not compiled) | not timed |

Notes:
- In sanitizer builds `KNOWN_ISSUE_UB` cases are `skip()`ped automatically (`support/TestHelpers.h:41`), so the `effectsForces` case is skipped there anyway; the exclusion is kept for consistency.
- Abort message: `/usr/include/c++/16/array:210: std::array<short,2>::operator[]: Assertion '__n < this->size()' failed.`

### Other P0 findings

- Two baseline case names contain a comma (`constant force, spring and damper are summed`, `set periodic stores magnitude, offset, phase and period`). doctest splits filters at commas, so these could only be excluded with a `?` wildcard in place of the comma. No action needed now.
- On an abort doctest's XML reporter still closes the document; the aborting case carries `<Exception crash="true">` and `test_case_success="true"` (because of `should_fail`). The checker treats this as a crash.

## Existing coverage and techniques (inventory)

Techniques already used in `Firmware/Tests/` (to be reused; see its README):
- **Link substitution**: fake platform in `platform/src/` implements HAL (`HAL_GPIO_*`, `HAL_UART_*`, `HAL_GetTick`), FreeRTOS (tasks, notifications, semaphores, `vTaskDelay`) and TinyUSB (`tud_cdc_n_write`, `tud_hid_n_report`), and the **`Flash_*` API itself** (map-backed, so `flash_helpers.cpp`/`eeprom.c` are not compiled).
- **Shadowed headers**: `platform/include/` replaces `main.h`, FreeRTOS headers, `target_constants.h`, `tusb_config.h`; `fakes/Axis.h` replaces the real `Axis.h` (include order matters).
- **Mocks of firmware interfaces**: `MockCommandHandler`, `MockCommandInterface`, `MockMotorDriver`, `MockEncoder`, `MockCANPort`; `PidHost` simulates the PID driver of the host; fixtures `CommandSystemFixture`, `FfbFixture`.
- **Simulated time/scheduler**: `HostPlatform::advanceMs`, `HostRtos::runUntilBlocked/runUntilIdle`; fixtures reset global state per test.
- **Markers**: `characterization:` names, `KNOWN_ISSUE(...)` (should_fail), `KNOWN_ISSUE_UB(...)` (skipped under sanitizers).
- README limitations to keep in mind: not compiled yet are `Axis`, the main classes, the real motor drivers/encoders and `SystemCommands`; USB descriptors are not parsed; no preemption.

Per-suite existing coverage (test case counts): CmdParser 39, CommandExecution/CommandHandler 34, StringCommandInterface/CDC/UART 41, HID_CommandInterface 22, CAN_CommandInterface 20, HidFFB 53, EffectsCalculator 86, Encoder/MotorDriver (mock interfaces) 17, platform self-test 6.

Coverage gaps (from gcov and the source map):
- **Not compiled at all**: Axis, AxesManager, SerialFFB, ErrorHandler commands, SystemCommands, flash_helpers, `eeprom.c`, all button/analog sources, non-driver encoders, CRC, FFBHIDMain + gamepad report, CanBridge, CanInputMain, CANPort2B, global_callbacks, SPI/I2C wrappers, ledEffects, voltagesense, USB descriptors, mainclass_chooser, TinyUSB device code.
- **Compiled but weakly covered**: ErrorHandler 13 %, Filters 28 % (only via effects), PersistentStorage 33 %, CanHandler/CAN 43-47 %, UsbHidHandler 45 %, ringbuffer/wrapper 50-59 % (wrap-around, overflow, peek paths), UART 57 %, CDCcomm 70 %, CommandHandler 84 % (help/info strings, class ID lookup), RTOS wrappers (`cthread`, `cmutex`, `csemaphore` 31-61 %).
- **EffectsCalculator**: high line coverage but no long-uptime tests (float timestamp precision, `micros()` wrap; see bug #4) and no test of `effectsForces` with a valid axis index > 0.

## Shared fakes and CMake changes (each is created in the phase that needs it, see TEST_PLAN.md)

Hard gate for every change: `ffb_unit_tests` must report 0 failed; on GCC 16 the whole executable gives 646 passed / 0 failed / 4 skipped with the exclusion (san 643/0/7, nodsp-san 645/0/5), and the 316 passed / 2 skipped baseline subset is checked by `tools/check_baseline.py`, in both DSP variants. Keep MinGW, presets and CI working.

| # | Fake / change | Location (`Firmware/Tests/`) | Purpose | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F1 | `FFB_TESTS_EXCLUDE` cache variable (default: the `effectsForces` case name) passed to `add_test` as `--test-case-exclude=` | `CMakeLists.txt` | Make `ctest` green and the exclusion visible in one place. Check CI impact first (older compilers do not abort on this case) | **done** `8861c911` (2026-10-21). Excluding the case on older compilers only skips a `KNOWN_ISSUE_UB` case; no CI runs the tests (P0) |
| F2 | Keep `ffb_unit_tests` exactly as is (same sources, same feature set, same fake `Axis.h`). Add `ffb_target_tests` (real `Axis.cpp`/`Axis.h`, `AxesManager`, `FFBHIDMain`, inputs, encoders, buses, CAN, system; no `fakes/Axis.h`), `ffb_rtos_tests` (the `cpp_freertos` wrappers), `ffb_flash_tests` (real `flash_helpers.cpp` + `eeprom.c` on fake HAL FLASH, without the `Flash_*` fake) and `ffb_usb_tests` (Tier B, real TinyUSB device code + fake USB controller). Firmware sources are compiled per executable; only the platform fakes are shared (static library). New cases go in separate directories (e.g. `cases_axis/`, `claude/cases_flash/`, `claude/cases_usb/`) outside the existing `cases/test_*.cpp` glob. The README says every `cases/test_*.cpp` is picked up automatically, so new executables must not share that directory | **done**: `ffb_target_tests` (P2), `ffb_flash_tests` (P3), `ffb_rtos_tests` (P8); `ffb_usb_tests` planned in P9 (on hold) |

None is hardware-only in the sense of the exclusion list.

- `Firmware/Tests/.gitignore` ignores `/specs/` (approved 2026-11-03); `Tests/Code_Coverage/shared/specs/` is the place for further spec files.

### Proposed CMake layout (D0 step 5, not applied)

`ffb_driver_tests` = `TARGET_FIRMWARE_SOURCES` + `TMC4671.cpp`, `TMC4671_configs.cpp`, `ODriveCAN.cpp`, `VescCAN.cpp`; `TARGET_PLATFORM_SOURCES` + `platform/src/host_driver_timers.cpp` (`htim6` like `MX_TIM6_Init`); feature header `platform/target_f407_drivers/target_constants.h` (= target header + `TMC4671DRIVER`, `ODRIVE`, `VESC`) before `platform/target_f407`; tests `claude/cases/driver/test_*.cpp` (not picked up by the non-recursive `cases/test_*.cpp` glob); own ctest entry and exclusion lists; added to `tools/run_all_tests.py` and `test_report`; separate driver tier in `coverage_report.py`. Full text: `../ffb-test-build/ws/d0/cmake_proposal.md`. Other executables unchanged (byte-identical compile commands, to be verified).

### Lead decisions needed

- (to me, before D2/D3) ODrive CAN protocol documentation for the targeted firmware version (code hints 0.5.4) and the VESC CAN packet list and scaling (code hints 5.3 build 51): still missing.

## P9 batch 0 (2026-11-02)

- **New executable `ffb_usb_tests`** (F2/F15): real TinyUSB device code (`tusb.c`, `tusb_fifo.c`, `usbd.c`, `usbd_control.c`, `cdc_device.c`, `hid_device.c`, `midi_device.c` (the F407 `tusb_config.h` sets `CFG_TUD_MIDI 1`; the firmware MIDI feature stays off)) with the `ffb_target_tests` firmware set and the real `global_callbacks.cpp`, `USBdevice`, `CDCcomm`, `UsbHidHandler`, descriptors. Defines `HOSTTEST_RTOS_EXT`, `HOSTTEST_REAL_TINYUSB`; `-fexceptions` for C (a parked resumable task may unwind through `usbd.c`). Own ctest entry, `FFB_USB_TESTS_EXCLUDE` / `_SANITIZE` (empty). The only shared change: `#ifndef HOSTTEST_REAL_TINYUSB` around the three TinyUSB models in `host_target.cpp` (all 240 existing TUs preprocess byte-identically). Weak-symbol overrides are a MinGW/PE concern (unverified).
- **F15 fake device controller** (`platform/src/host_dcd.cpp`, `support/HostDcd.h`), modelled on TinyUSB's dwc2 port (the F407's DCD, cited per function) and USB 2.0: init/connect, bus reset, SETUP, OUT/IN transactions with NAK/STALL/ZLP rules, SET_ADDRESS timing, suspend/resume/SOF, remote wakeup, endpoint open/stall/clear, events in ISR context; unmodelled cases recorded as faults. Host side `support/UsbHost.h` (control transfers, enumeration, CDC and HID helpers).
- **OSAL:** TinyUSB's unchanged `osal_freertos.h` on the F11 fake FreeRTOS, via TinyUSB's `CFG_TUSB_OS_INC_PATH` hook (`platform/usb/tusb_config.h` -> `hosttest_osal/`): static create functions (F407 `configSUPPORT_STATIC_ALLOCATION 1`) and an `xQueueReset` that accepts a semaphore. `tud_task` runs deterministically on the test thread.
- **Fixture** `support/UsbFixture.h` (F407 FFBWheel USB setup, resumable CDC/command threads, end-of-test checks, `REQUIRE_USB_MODELLED()`). 14 smoke cases (`UsbSetup`): enumeration with the real descriptors, SET_ADDRESS timing, strings, CDC line coding, a command over CDC and its reply, HID IN/OUT/feature reports, stall, resume; KNOWN_ISSUE bug 42 confirmed on the real stack; new vendor **V21**.
- **Python harness** `Firmware/Tests/Code_Coverage/claude/python/` (stdlib `unittest`, temp dirs only, no `__pycache__`): `generate_memory.py` reproduces the committed `eeprom_addresses.c/.h` byte for byte; `dwc2_info.py` decodes the F407 FS core (its unused `click` import and `pandas` are stubbed). ctest entry `python_helper_tests` when Python 3 is found: ctest now runs 7 entries.
- Gate: ctest 7/7 and baseline OK in all 6 dirs + cov; existing executables unchanged (compile commands identical; verified by the main agent); `ffb_usb_tests` 14/0/0 everywhere; python 5/5; no findings, no test-file warnings. Tier B coverage after the smoke tests 755/1,572 (48 %); in-scope firmware unchanged at 98.9 %.

## P6 batch 0 (2026-10-26)

- **Resumable tasks** in the fake kernel (`host_freertos.cpp`, `HostRtos::makeResumable(handle|name)`, opt-in): `EncoderBissC` and `MtEncoderSPI` wait twice per loop (`requestSem.Take()`, DMA, `WaitForNotification()`), which the restart-from-`Run()` model cannot test. A resumable task runs on its own `std::thread` with a strict mutex/condition-variable hand-over (one thread runs at a time, deterministic); blocking calls with `portMAX_DELAY` park until the condition holds, finite timeouts park once then time out, `vTaskDelete` of a parked task unwinds and joins. Without `makeResumable` nothing changes. Needs `std::thread`, which was already required before P6 (`finishesWithin` in `TestHelpers.h`, `Threads::Threads` linked): the MinGW requirement is unchanged (posix thread model, or win32 threads with GCC 13+); unverified on Windows. macOS secondary threads have 512 KiB stacks.
- Encoder-mode timer fake: `HostTarget::encoderTimerCount(htim, counts)` (counts only with CEN, UIF at ARR->0 / 0->ARR, update branch of `HAL_TIM_IRQHandler` in ISR context), `timerCounter`/`timerSetCounter`, `encoderIndexPulse()` (EXTI falling edge per `MX_GPIO_Init`). `HostTarget::spiFrameBytes` (16-64 bit frames, MSB first). Unmodelled: DIR bit, input filter/polarity, CNT above ARR (recorded), UIF pending when UIE is enabled later.
- Reset of the static `inUse` flags of the 4 encoders; `AxisFixture::selectFirmwareEncoder(enctype, i)`; `mocks/MockSPIDevice.h`, `mocks/MockI2CDevice.h`. No new HAL functions needed; no CMake change.
- 14 smoke cases (`EncoderSetup`). Gate: ctest 4/4 and baseline OK in all 6 dirs, `ffb_unit_tests`/`ffb_flash_tests` compile commands identical, `ffb_target_tests` 1,084/0/8 (default, clang23), 1,085/0/8 (nodsp), 979 + 113 (san, clang23-san), 980 + 113 (nodsp-san); no findings; 30 sanitizer runs and random orders green. Verified by the main agent.
- Suspected bugs from code reading, handed to the module agents (to be confirmed by tests): `EncoderSSI.cpp:124` raw decode, `EncoderSSI.h:73` uninitialised `mode`, `EncoderSSI.cpp:110` AMT23 check-bit test, `EncoderLocal.cpp:85` `% cpr` with cpr 0, `MtEncoderSPI.cpp:113-114` / `301-302` clip-to-size, `MtEncoderSPI.cpp:335` unchecked mode, `EncoderBissC.cpp:252-264` `__builtin_clzll(0)`, unchecked `bits`, `hasFreePins` in BissC/SSI (bug 67 pattern), MtEncoderSPI deleted mid-DMA keeps the port semaphore.

Defines `HOSTTEST_FLASH_HAL` (target `main.h` includes `host_flash_hal.h`, values from the ST headers) and `HOSTTEST_REAL_FLASH_HELPERS` (the map-backed `Flash_*`/`OTP_*` fake in `host_platform.cpp` is left out). Exclusion lists `FFB_FLASH_TESTS_EXCLUDE` / `_SANITIZE` (empty). No CMSIS-DSP.
- Firmware compiled: `flash_helpers.cpp`, `eeprom.c` (tier A), `eeprom_addresses.c`, `PersistentStorage.cpp`; for linking `cmutex.cpp`, `csemaphore.cpp`, `UART.cpp`, `UartHandler.cpp`. No stubs.
- F9 fake HAL FLASH (`platform/src/host_flash.cpp`, `support/HostFlash.h`) modelled on `stm32f4xx_hal_flash(_ex).c` and RM0090: LOCK bit, program = old & data with 0->1 requests recorded, PGSERR when locked, PGAERR across a 128-bit row, pending SR flags, sector erase (sectors 0-3), erase while locked ignored, OTP 16x32 bytes + lock bytes (WRPERR on locked block), cache macros counted; injections (error flags, HAL_TIMEOUT/HAL_BUSY, unlock failure) and **power loss** (cut after N programs with torn-bit mask, partial erase), recording per sector, `unexpected` accesses fail the test.
- **Fixed-address memory:** 64 KiB mapped at 0x08000000 and at 0x1FFF0000 at startup (POSIX `mmap` hint + `MAP_FIXED_NOREPLACE`, never `MAP_FIXED`; Windows `VirtualAlloc` at the fixed base), abort with a message if unavailable. Verified on Linux x86-64 incl. ASan (LowMem 0x0-0x7fff7fff is application memory). **Not verified on Windows; not built on macOS** (4 GiB `__PAGEZERO`; decision 2026-10-24).
- `support/FlashFixture.h`: resets fake flash, injections, PersistentStorage lists/startup flag, `eeprom.c` `DataVar`; `init()`, `reboot()`, page image helpers (`writePage`, `fillPage`, `pageRecords`, ...), `REQUIRE_FLASH_ACCESSES_MODELLED()`.
- 9 smoke cases (`FlashSetup`), incl. a power loss at every program step of a page transfer (recovers).
- Gate: ctest 4/4 in all 6 dirs, baseline OK, `ffb_unit_tests` and `ffb_target_tests` unchanged, `ffb_flash_tests` 9/0/0 everywhere, no sanitizer findings, no warnings from test files (firmware `-Wint-to-pointer-cast` in `eeprom.c`/`flash_helpers.cpp`: 32-bit addresses as pointers, host only). Verified again by the main agent.

- Notes for module agents: 1-axis `HidFFB` default direction mask 0x02 (FFBWheel sets 0x04 in `usbInit()`); `FFBHIDMain::Run` is re-entered by the fake kernel (timer start returns `HAL_ERROR` when BUSY, like the HAL); motor start needs `tud_connected()`; fade-in 1 s at 1 kHz; axes inverted by default; main-class axis via `CommandHandler::getHandlerFromClassName("axis", 0)`.

## P1 plan (2026-10-22)

Executable `ffb_unit_tests`, new `cases/test_*.cpp` files only. Gaps taken from the P0 coverage build (never-run functions per file). ## P1 results (2026-10-22/23)

Commit `a79ead3a` (tests and fake changes, before review): 329 new cases. After the review fixes: **332 new cases** in 15 new files (C 62, E 114, others unchanged); no existing test file changed.

| Group | Files | Cases | Own mutation spot-check |
|---|---|---|---|
| A command | `test_cmdparser_gaps.cpp`, `test_command_handler_gaps.cpp`, `test_hid_command_interface_gaps.cpp` | 54 | 38/39 own files, 39/39 full suite |
| B ffb | `test_effects_calculator_gaps.cpp`, `test_hid_ffb_gaps.cpp` | 30 | 23/26 own, 24/26 full (2 equivalent) |
| C errors/storage | `test_error_handler.cpp`, `test_persistent_storage.cpp` | 61 | 28/29 (survivor equivalent on the fake: `xTaskResumeFromISR` vs `vTaskResume`) |
| D filters/small | `test_filters.cpp`, `test_device_interfaces_gaps.cpp`, `test_rtos_wrappers.cpp` | 72 | 55/58 (3 equivalent) |
| E transport | `test_uart.cpp`, `test_ringbuffer.cpp`, `test_can_handler.cpp`, `test_usb_hid_handler.cpp`, `test_cdc.cpp` | 112 | 63/65 (1 equivalent, 1 hidden by FIFO clamping of the fake) |
| **Total** | | **329 (332 after review)** | **207/217 = 95 %** (target 80 %); after review C's survivor at `ErrorHandler.cpp:177` is killed too (fake records the resume API) |

All 6 review survivors (CmdParser:53, :156, EffectsCalculator:235, :1275, HidFFB:423, HidCommandInterface:92) are now killed.

Shared fake changes (additive, needed by the tests):
- `platform/src/host_freertos.cpp`, `support/HostRtos.h`: `HostRtos::findTask(name)`, `isSuspended(task)`, `semaphoreCount()`; `vTaskSuspend(NULL)` suspends the caller and a task that suspends itself now blocks like on the target (needed for `ErrorPrinter::Run`; no other compiled code suspends itself).
- `platform/src/host_platform.cpp`, `support/HostPlatform.h`: UART call counters per HAL function, last timeout/size, `rxData` for blocking `HAL_UART_Receive`, injectable results (`initResult`, `txResult`, `rxArmResult`, `abortResult`); `HAL_UART_Receive_DMA` arms the buffer like `_IT`, `HAL_UART_AbortReceive` disarms it. Defaults keep the old behaviour.
- After review: C added `HostRtos::resumeStats()` (counts `vTaskResume`, `xTaskResumeFromISR`, and `vTaskResume` called in ISR context, which is only recorded); `xTaskResumeFromISR` no longer calls `vTaskResume`. E made the UART fake complete a receive only after the armed size (like the HAL), `AbortReceive` resets the count, and documented the remaining fake limits in `HostPlatform.h`.
- Fixes during integration: two unused helper functions (`-Wunused-function` in `test_can_handler.cpp`, `test_ringbuffer.cpp`). No warnings from the new files with GCC 16 or clang 23.

Bus-off, error warning/passive, LEC events and FIFO1 frames raise no interrupt of their own; `HAL_CAN_IRQHandler` checks every flag, so they are handled only at the next CAN1_TX or CAN1_RX0 interrupt (Tx completion/abort or a FIFO0 frame), nothing while the bus is idle. | 319 | ST HAL UART: test limits | `test_hal_uart_*.cpp` | Not tested: Register / UnRegister callbacks (compiled out, `USE_HAL_UART_REGISTER_CALLBACKS` 0), synchronous / smartcard / IrDA / hardware flow control (CTS / RTS), LIN break detection, noise detection details, TE / RE edges mid-frame, idle preamble, wake-up from low power, interrupt delivery; four lines unreachable on the host (`ReceiveToIdle_IT` / `_DMA` `status = HAL_ERROR` after an interrupt between two statements, the IDLE clear at the last frame of `UART_Receive_IT`); the model's frame time (default 1 tick), the IDLE rule and the address-mark MSB with parity are assumptions; the model stops UART4 / UART5 bits the RM0090 marks unavailable (CLKEN, CPOL, CPHA, LBCL, CTSIE, CTSE, RTSE, SCEN, NACK) but not STOP 0.5 / 1.5 or GTPR | open |
| 320 | ST HAL I2C: init, deinit, timing | `test_hal_i2c_init.cpp` (II1-II7) | `characterization:` a failed Init (PCLK1 too low) leaves state BUSY with PE 0 after the SWRST and MspInit (a second Init then skips MspInit); CCR rounds up for non-exact quotients (Sm, Fm duty 2, Fm duty 16/9); a CCR that does not fit 12 bits is masked and raised to the minimum 4 (2 MHz / 244 Hz) or wraps (42 MHz / 5 kHz gives 104, no error); DeInit while busy still resets the handle | open |
| 321 | ST HAL I2C: controller, blocking mode | `test_hal_i2c_poll.cpp` (IP1-IP7) | `characterization:` Size 0 is accepted (address phase and STOP); a timeout returns `HAL_ERROR` with `HAL_I2C_ERROR_TIMEOUT` (`HAL_I2C_WRONG_START` when the START wait times out with START still set; `HAL_TIMEOUT` only from `IsDeviceReady`); the 25 ms BUSY wait returns `HAL_BUSY` (the 25 ms is the HAL constant); the blocking functions never look at ARLO or BERR; `Trials` 0 tries once; a `MemAddSize` that is not 8 or 16 bit is sent as 16 bit; state RESET or a locked handle gives `HAL_BUSY`; STOPF in the RXNE wait ends the call with `HAL_ERROR`; an 8-bit `Mem_Read` with a nacked address byte goes on with the repeated start (TXE stays 1 after the first DR write, RM0090 27.6.6 note); a slow-bus `Mem_Read` with a nacked LSB ends in the read-phase ADDR wait; timeout sweeps check only clean-end invariants in the undetermined middle region | open |
| 322 | ST HAL I2C: target mode, listen, address callbacks | `test_hal_i2c_slave.cpp` (IS1-IS5) | `characterization:` a stop before `Size` bytes ends `Slave_Receive` with `HAL_ERROR` and ErrorCode NONE (blocking) or AF (IT); ACK stays set after an address-wait timeout; a controller nack before all bytes were given ends `Slave_Transmit` with AF; the match code of a general call is OwnAddress1; a nack in listen mode gives ErrorCallback (AF) then ListenCplt; blocking `Slave_Receive` neither reports nor clears OVR with NOSTRETCH; the direction constants (`I2C_DIRECTION_TRANSMIT` = controller writes) come from the header and ST usage, UM1725 says only "Write / Read"; ADDR is set when the address matches while ACK = 0 (RM0090 27.3.2) | open |
| 323 | ST HAL I2C: interrupt mode, IRQ handlers, abort | `test_hal_i2c_irq.cpp` (IT1-IT5) | `characterization:` IT start functions return `HAL_BUSY` for a locked READY handle and for a busy bus (ErrorCode TIMEOUT); `Mem_Write_IT` / `Master_Receive_IT` / `Mem_Read_IT` of Size 0; POS stays set after a 2-byte receive until the next start; `Master_Abort_IT` ignores `DevAddress`, leaves PE 0, ErrorCode NONE, state READY with mode MASTER / MEM; STOPF before `Size` bytes gives ErrorCallback with AF after storing data in DR; simultaneous BERR / ARLO / AF / OVR give all four bits and one callback; all interrupt enables are cleared after an error; AF ends the listen state (ErrorCallback then ListenCplt); ITBUFEN stays set after a 2-byte receive that reaches BTF; a 1-byte receive whose STOP never completes ends with TIMEOUT | open |
| 324 | ST HAL I2C: sequential transfer functions | `test_hal_i2c_seq.cpp` (IQ1-IQ4) | `characterization:` a NEXT_FRAME / LAST_FRAME as the first call generates a START; after a transfer error the next NEXT_FRAME starts with S; `I2C_NO_OPTION_FRAME` (private) behaves like FIRST_AND_LAST_FRAME; a master receive of Size 0 reads one byte; a slave receive frame cut short by a stop ends with AF, one ErrorCallback and ListenCplt; OTHER_FRAME / OTHER_AND_LAST_FRAME are rewritten to FIRST_FRAME / FIRST_AND_LAST_FRAME; a LAST_FRAME call on a held bus does not check BUSY; raw `PreviousState` values (0x11, 0x12, 0x21, 0); a 1-byte FIRST_AND_NEXT_FRAME receive never completes and reads past `Size` (first proposed as a vendor bug, withdrawn: UM1725 does not promise a completion callback for it) | open |
| 325 | ST HAL I2C: DMA mode | `test_hal_i2c_dma.cpp` (ID1-ID4) | `characterization:` Size 0 DMA calls start no stream; a missing DMA handle returns `HAL_ERROR` with `HAL_I2C_ERROR_DMA_PARAM` and the slave functions leave state LISTEN; a failing `HAL_DMA_Start_IT` gives `HAL_ERROR` with ERROR_DMA; a receive completing with an ErrorCode set calls the error callback; slave stop / nack early ends with AF (error callback after the DMA abort); a DMA FIFO error is ignored but `I2C_DMAError` has already cleared the complete callbacks, so the transfer never completes; an I2C error during DMA aborts the stream first and `ErrorCallback` follows the abort; a stream that cannot be aborted disables PE and calls the abort callback directly; FIRST_FRAME receive sets LAST / clears ACK in the ADDR handler; after a slave sequential Rx to Tx switch the abort callback sets state LISTEN while the Tx stream runs | open |

Model decisions the manual leaves open: the first DR write goes to the shift register and TXE stays 1; TXE is 1 right after ADDR of a transmitter; a data byte is held until ADDR is cleared; a byte left in DR when START / STOP is pending is dropped; POS = 1 acks byte k by the ACK value sampled at byte k-1; ACK is sampled at the 9th clock, before DR is read; SR1 read then SR2 read clears ADDR even if ADDR was set between; SWRST resets state, SR1, SR2 and CR1 only; the peer sends 0xFF after its queue | open |
| 327 | ST HAL CAN: lifecycle | `test_hal_can_init.cpp` (CI1-CI6) | `characterization:` the Init, Start, Stop, WakeUp timeouts return `HAL_ERROR` with `HAL_CAN_ERROR_TIMEOUT` (Init leaves state ERROR, a second Init then skips MspInit; WakeUp leaves the state alone); failing calls OR their bit into the existing ErrorCode (NOT_READY, NOT_STARTED, NOT_INITIALIZED); DeInit stops first and MspDeInit sees state READY; the 10 ms timeout limit is the HAL constant (acknowledge at 11 ticks of sync delay passes, 12 times out); INAK and SLAK follow INRQ and SLEEP independently (RM0090 32.4.3 only says to clear SLEEP as well; HAL_CAN_Init relies on it) | open |
| 328 | ST HAL CAN: filter configuration and matching | `test_hal_can_filter.cpp` (CF1-CF4) | `characterization:` the 16-bit scale register packing (the HAL puts IdLow / MaskIdLow in FiR1 and IdHigh / MaskIdHigh in FiR2 although UM1725 calls the High fields "first"; RM0090 Figure 342 makes FiR1 filter n: arguable, not promoted to a bug); an undefined scale leaves the registers unchanged; undefined mode is list, undefined FIFO is FIFO 1, undefined activation is disabled; bank 28 writes beyond the 28 banks (a model fault, cleared by the test); ConfigFilter in a wrong state returns `HAL_ERROR` with NOT_INITIALIZED; filter numbering restarts per FIFO and per instance (model decision, Medium) | open |
| 329 | ST HAL CAN: mailboxes and FIFOs | `test_hal_can_txrx.cpp` (CT1-CT4) | `characterization:` AddTxMessage with all mailboxes full and GetRxMessage on an empty FIFO return `HAL_ERROR` with `HAL_CAN_ERROR_PARAM` (UM1725 names no code); a received DLC of 9..15 is reported as 8; the TX time stamp is the value at frame completion (model); DATA6 / DATA7 are not replaced by the time stamp with TGT and TTCM (model) | open |
| 330 | ST HAL CAN: notifications and IRQ handler | `test_hal_can_irq.cpp` (CQ1-CQ5) | `characterization:` IER bits RM0090 marks reserved are not stored; ALST and TERR together report only `HAL_CAN_ERROR_TX_ALST`; callback order inside one handler call (TX0-2, FIFO0 full / pending, FIFO1, sleep, wake-up, error) and the pending callback runs again on every call while FMP != 0; an ESR LEC of 7 (set by software) maps to no error code and is cleared; ErrorCode only accumulates; Activate / Deactivate in the wrong state return `HAL_ERROR` with NOT_INITIALIZED (also in SLEEP_ACTIVE / SLEEP_PENDING) | open |
| 331 | ST HAL CAN: test limits and model decisions | `test_hal_can_*.cpp`, `HalCan.{h,c}` | Not tested: Register / UnRegister callbacks (compiled out, `USE_HAL_CAN_REGISTER_CALLBACKS` 0), the CAN3 and single-instance branches (preprocessor), bit timing, time-triggered mode beyond the time stamp, bus-off recovery (ABOM), error counter evolution, interrupt delivery. Model decisions the manual leaves open: arbitration key = base identifier, IDE, extended identifier (equal keys: lower mailbox first); a message matching filters of both FIFOs takes the better class then the lower number; FULL / FOVR clear only by software; TIME captured at completion; the 16-bit mask / list register format; the INAK / SLAK independence; `hostCanSetPollReads` lets the counter loop of `HAL_CAN_WakeUp` see time pass | open |

New tests that describe unclear behaviour use the `characterization:` name prefix and get a row here. Tests that describe intended behaviour but fail use `KNOWN_ISSUE` and get a row under "Suspected firmware bugs".


## G1 review result (2026-11-09)

- **Reviewers:** two fresh Sonnet `driver-test-reviewer` agents (float side: f32/f64/f16/helpers; fixed point: q31/q15), scratch trees under `../ffb-test-build/audit/g1f-*` and `g1q-*`. Repo untouched by them.
- **Mutation check:** 96 mutants of CMSIS-DSP sources (58 float, 38 fixed point), 91 killed. Survivors: 3 perturbations inside ARM's own tolerance (atan2_f32 coefficient, atan2_f16 pi/2, vlog_f16 slope), the f32 sin table wrap (killed under ASan only; a value test added), R31b/R15b (index -8 read for input 0, killed under ASan only), L31d (out-of-domain vlog, excluded by decision L8), A31e (equivalent).
- **Fixes applied:** q31 divide fuzz drew two `lcg` calls in one expression (unspecified order) now separate statements; q31 vlog "at most one LSB" now asserts 1; added q31 divide INT32_MIN operand and INT32_MIN/0 tests, `sh == 0` check, q31 sqrt strided sweep, q31 vlog normalisation-boundary sweep; corrected vendor line numbers in B-DFQ31-1; f32 sin/cos table-wrap case; f16 atan2 range margin; renamed two abs tests; f16 sin/cos placeholder is tagged `[doc ...]`.
- **New finding V42** (`arm_sqrt_q31`, see bug table; the sweep I added found it, then a temporary full-range scratch test measured it; the scratch file was deleted).
- **Low items (done at my request, 2026-11-09):** f16 C4 edges added (vexp +-inf/NaN and 11.0, vinverse NaN/+-inf and overflow, sqrt +inf, atan2 huge/tiny ratio; all pass), f64 hand-derived constants for vexp/vlog, f32 sin/cos sweep 7..1000 rad with a growing phase-error bound, FPSCR.FZ remark in the f32 sqrt test, position dependent q31 sin/cos bound (t(1-t)/2 h^2 (|f|+h)). All passed on the first run; no new finding.
- **Gate (2026-11-09):** default, ASan+UBSan, clang 23: ctest 11 of 11; DSP off 10 of 10; `--coverage` build 11 of 11; baseline check OK (default and san, 340 new cases, 0 failed). `ffb_dsp_tests` after the low items: 232 cases default and clang, 204 run (28 skipped) in the sanitizer build, 0 failed; ctest default/san/clang 11 of 11, DSP off 10 of 10, baseline OK. No compiler warning in the DSP test files (gcc, clang).
- **Coverage:** FastMath 443 / 451 lines (98.2 %), 26 / 26 functions; BasicMath helpers 62 / 62.


## G2 review result (2026-11-09)

- **Scope:** Interpolation (linear and bilinear f32 / q31 / q15 / q7 / f16, spline) and Controller (pid f32 / q31 / q15, sin_cos f32 / q31, Clarke / Park and inverses f32 / q31). Files: `claude/cases_dsp/test_dsp_interp_{fixed,f32,f16}.cpp`, `test_dsp_controller_{pid,sincos,transforms}.cpp`. Work orders `Tests/Code_Coverage/claude/charters/dsp/G2_WORK_ORDERS.md` (the sine spline test of ARM uses `ARM_SPLINE_NATURAL`, not PARABOLIC_RUNOUT as the work order said). ARM has patterns only for linear / bilinear / three spline sets; everything else is DEF.
- **Writers:** IQ, IF, IH, CP, CT (Sonnet). **Reviewers:** two fresh Sonnet agents (interpolation: 116 mutants, 109 killed, 4 equivalent, 3 inside the derived bounds; controller: 95 mutants, 78 killed, 17 survivors of which 9 were real gaps, all closed by the fixes; full 2^32 sweep of `arm_sin_cos_q31` and 40M-input sweeps of Clarke / Park q31 and 16M of `arm_sin_cos_f32` found no defect beyond V50 / V51 / V52).
- **Review fixes applied:** f16 sample-35 rationale corrected (V-less, NEEDS REVIEW 203); guard-intact names and suite name fixed; f32 bilinear C5 bound 16 to 42 ulps; float-cast cases made plain tests; sin_cos q31 saturation scans added (the old saturation case tested nothing), sweep stride 8191, tighter bounds; second `arm_pid_q31` wrap site (V47) and the init partial-sum clipping (V48) found by review; flag-0 init gain checks for q31 / q15; one-sided floor bounds for PID and Park.
- **New vendor findings:** V43-V52 (V43 / V44 q31 interpolation shifts, V45 bilinear truncation f16 and f32, V46 / V48 pid init q31, V47 pid_q31 wrap, V49 / V50 sin_cos_q31, V51 clarke_q31, V52 park q31). B-DIF-1 (spline reads one float past the query block) is the existing finding 2, not a new number. NEEDS REVIEW 200-208.

No compiler warning in the DSP test files.

## G4 review result (2026-11-09)

- **Scope:** Filtering part 1: biquad df1 (f32, f16, q31, q15, fast q31 / q15, 32x64 q31), df2T (f32, f64, f16), stereo df2T (f32, f16), FIR f32 / f64 / f16 / q31 / q15 / q7 and fast q31 / q15, decimation f32 / f64 / q31 / q15 (+ fast), interpolation f32 / q31 / q15, all inits. Files: `claude/cases_dsp/test_dsp_filt_{biquad_f32,biquad_f64,biquad_f16,biquad_q,fir_float,fir_f16,fir_q,decim_float,decim_q}.cpp`. Work orders `Tests/Code_Coverage/claude/charters/dsp/G4_WORK_ORDERS.md`. ARM ids covered: BIQUAD f32 5, f16 5, f64 2, q31 2, q15 1; FIR f32 / f64 / f16 / q31 / q15 / q7 one each; DECIM f32 / f64 decimate and interpolate f32, q31 and q15 decimate and interpolate. ARM has no id for the fast variants (covered by DEF and C5), the f64 interpolate does not exist (`#if 0`), nothing else was disabled.
- **Writers:** FB32, FB6416, FBQ, FIRF, FIRQ, DECF, DECQ (Sonnet); the first wave was interrupted by an API rate limit and resumed (FBQ rewrote its file from scratch). **Reviewers:** four fresh Sonnet agents (biquad float + decimate float: 70 mutants, 0 real survivors; biquad q + FIR float / f16: 40 mutants, 3 survivors of which 1 real; FIR q: 51 mutants, 4 survivors; decimate q: 40 mutants, 5 survivors); gaps closed by the writers; the new tests were re-checked with scratch mutants by the writers (not by the reviewers).
- **Lead decisions:** (1) DOC contradictions on q31 narrowing (saturated vs wraps) are `characterization:` tests plus NEEDS REVIEW, not KNOWN_ISSUE (B-DDECQ-1..3 and B-DFBQ-5 dropped; B-DFBQ-5 also violated the DOC input range). (2) The interpolate init error code is a DOC defect, `characterization:` asserting LENGTH_ERROR. (3) q15 numTaps = 2 is not a defect (ARM uses it).
- **Review fixes (all files):** `dsp::GuardedBuffer` keeps its guard words in the same allocation, so ASan cannot see a state overrun; every file now runs the main tests on a tight exact-size heap state buffer and keeps the guard tests (verified with scratch overrun mutants, ASan aborts in each file). A short-circuiting init that crashed the run on failure (decim float) now reports a failure. Long double epsilon replaces a hard-coded 2^-64. q7 tail saturation, fast q31 tie, unrolled-path wrap tests added. Bitwise partition equality replaced by derived bounds.
- **New vendor findings:** V61-V69 (see the bug table; mostly UB-hidden left shifts of negative values in q31 / q15 kernels and the q15 host SIMD emulation, one defect KNOWN_ISSUE: `arm_fir_init_q15` accepts an odd numTaps); NEEDS REVIEW 218-227.
- **Gate (2026-11-09):** `ffb_dsp_tests` 1844 cases (1 skipped: the INT64_MIN / -1 crash case) default and clang 23, 1635 run (210 skipped, UB-hidden and UB cases) in the sanitizer build, 0 failed (the failed assertions are the expected KNOWN_ISSUE ones); ctest default / san / clang 11 of 11, DSP off 10 of 10, `--coverage` 11 of 11; baseline check OK (default and san, 340 new cases, 0 failed). Mutants of the reviewers were not re-run after the writers' fixes except as stated.
- **Coverage (gcc, gcov):** FilteringFunctions 3243 / 3243 lines (100 %, 52 / 52 functions with data; 64 in-scope files not linked), Statistics 1799 / 1799, FastMath 443 / 451, Controller 97 / 97, Interpolation 233 / 233, BasicMath helpers 62 / 62; total 5877 / 5885 (99.9 %), 192 of 258 in-scope source files linked and measured.
- **Not verified:** Windows / macOS / ARM target behaviour, the Cortex-M SIMD and MVE paths, FMA contraction on the target, the target results of the UB-hidden cases.

## G5 review result (2026-11-10)

- **Scope:** Filtering part 2: convolution, partial convolution and correlation (f32 / f64 / f16 / q31 / q15 / q7 with fast, opt and fast_opt variants), Levinson-Durbin (f32, f16, q31), LMS and norm-LMS (f32, q15, q31) with `arm_recip_q31` / `arm_recip_q15`, FIR and IIR lattices (f32, q15, q31), sparse FIR (f32, q31, q15, q7). Files: `claude/cases_dsp/test_dsp_filt_{conv_float,conv_f16,conv_q31,conv_q15,conv_q7,lms,lattice,sparse}.cpp`. Work orders `Tests/Code_Coverage/claude/charters/dsp/G5_WORK_ORDERS.md`. ARM's MISC suites cover only Levinson-Durbin, correlate, conv and partial conv (all their ids are run, including the disabled F64 ids that map to no library function); LMS, lattices and sparse FIR have no ARM test and are DEF-only.
- **Writers:** CVF, CVQ31, CVQ15, CVQ7, LMS, LATT, SPAR (Sonnet); interrupted twice by an API rate limit and once by a connection error (CVF, CVQ31, CVQ15, CVQ7 rewrote or finished their files after the resume). **Reviewers:** four fresh Sonnet agents (conv float / f16 / q31: about 70 mutants; conv q15 / q7: 34 mutants; LMS + sparse: about 70 mutants; lattices: 56 mutants, 0 survivors); the writers closed the gaps; the one open mutant claim (A16, rounding instead of floor in the fast q15 functions) was confirmed killed by measurement in a scratch tree.
- **Lead decisions:** (1) the q31 narrowing wrap-versus-saturated DOC contradiction stays `characterization:` + NEEDS REVIEW (same rule as G4), reviewers argued for KNOWN_ISSUE (NEEDS REVIEW 229, undecided). (2) The target path (`ARM_MATH_DSP`) is not run in the suite, findings from reviewer scratch builds are recorded as NEEDS REVIEW 231 and in V71 / V75 / V76 without tests. (3) Levinson q31 keeps ARM's 30 LSB (charter L4). (4) B-DLMS-7 (norm_q15 energy wrap) is kept as a KNOWN_ISSUE_UB: the coefficient moves the wrong way under any reading of the DOC.
- **Review fixes (all files):** heap-dependent `KNOWN_ISSUE_UB` cases replaced by deterministic ones with a poisoned element behind the input (a case whose only failing assertion depends on stale heap content is not allowed); the false partial_fast q15 window limit replaced by `first <= max(A,B)`; tight exact-size heap buffers for outputs, state and scratch in every file (verified with scratch overrun mutants); `inputOk` tautology fixed in the sparse tests; sweeps whose exact model rests on an unspecified DOC detail renamed `characterization:`; exact non-negative oracles added for the fast q15 functions; `__SIZEOF_INT128__` guard; ARM line citations corrected.
- **New vendor findings:** V70-V85 (see the bug table); NEEDS REVIEW 228-237. V73 (fast q15 narrowing, 6 plain KNOWN_ISSUE cases) is the main plain defect; V83 (lms_q15 window wrap), V84 (norm_q15 energy wrap) and V81 (sparse numTaps = 1 crash) are new unambiguous-DOC findings; the rest is UB in the host SIMD emulation and unrolled kernels.
- **Gate (2026-11-10):** `ffb_dsp_tests` 2485 cases (5 skipped: the INT64_MIN / -1 crash case and the 4 sparse numTaps = 1 crash cases) default and clang 23, 2087 run (403 skipped: UB-hidden, UB and crash cases) in the sanitizer build, 0 failed (the failed assertions are the expected KNOWN_ISSUE ones); ctest default / san / clang 11 of 11, DSP off 10 of 10, `--coverage` 11 of 11; baseline check OK (default and san, 340 new cases, 0 failed). No compiler warning from the DSP test files (gcc).
- **Coverage (gcc, gcov):** FilteringFunctions 7754 / 7754 lines (100 %, 116 / 116 functions with data), Statistics 1799 / 1799, FastMath 443 / 451, Controller 97 / 97, Interpolation 233 / 233, BasicMath helpers 62 / 62; total 10388 / 10396 (99.9 %), 256 source files linked and measured, 2 without data (CommonTables). Coverage counts the host-compiled branches only: the `ARM_MATH_DSP` unrolled kernels of the conv / correlate family are not measured (NEEDS REVIEW 231).

- **Specifications:** the owner supplied `TMCL_reference.pdf` (v2.22, 2008), `TMC4671-TOSV-REF_HW_FW_manual_Rev1.1.pdf` and `TMC4671-LEV-REF_datasheet_hw1.00_fw1.00_rev1.00.pdf` (2021, same frame / reply / status tables, so those parts are current); they specify the frame, the checksum, the reply layout, the status codes and the commands 10 (GGP), 15 (GIO) and 136 (version); the opcodes 143 / 146 / 148 are in none of them. Specified parts are plain tests, the rest `characterization:`, the silent drops and invalid status bytes are `KNOWN_ISSUE` by the owner's decision (charter L1, L5).
- **Writers:** TDC (commands, 56 cases), TDF (frames, 85), TDL (lifecycle, 23), plus the 3 T1 setup cases (Sonnet). **Reviewers:** three fresh Sonnet agents (lifecycle: 31 mutants, 27 killed; frames: 87 mutants, 4 survivors; commands: 40 mutants, 37 killed), all gaps and findings fixed by the writers and re-checked with scratch mutants; the two surviving equivalent mutants (`turn` before `startMotor` from Shutdown, `setAddress` removed) are documented in the files.
- **Lead decisions (Sonnet as lead, as the owner asked, 2026-11-10):** (1) invalid TMCL status bytes in the GGP / GIO (64) and 143 (40) replies are `KNOWN_ISSUE`; for the undocumented opcode 143 only membership in the valid status set {100, 1..6} is asserted. (2) The first `torque set` from Shutdown is `characterization:` (D1 R62 makes a deferred write a legal reading), not a `KNOWN_ISSUE`. (3) Binary version layout versus TMCL §3.35 stays `characterization:` (ambiguous byte numbering). (4) Named driver states are not reachable through the public interface; no firmware edit, no friend access. (5) Mode 7 is `prbs_position_mode` per DS 0x63 (valid, not a defect, the charter's "unnamed" claim was wrong); the charter's `TDH` line numbers were stale (limits at `TMCDebugBridge.h:55-63`, `tmcpids` at `:44-53`).
- **New firmware findings:** 194-200 (see the bug table): five silent or invalid TMCL replies and the `mode set` negative-value aliasing, all `KNOWN_ISSUE`; NEEDS REVIEW 238-243.
- **Gate (2026-11-10):** `ffb_driver_tests` 1097 cases (933 after T1: +56 commands, +85 frames, +23 lifecycle = +164; the 3 setup cases were added in T1) default and clang 23, 1087 run (19 skipped) in the sanitizer build, 0 failed (the failed assertions are the expected `KNOWN_ISSUE` ones); ctest default / san / clang 11 of 11, DSP off 10 of 10, `--coverage` 11 of 11; baseline check OK (default and san, 340 new cases, 0 failed); the other five executables have identical compile flags and object lists as before (T1 comparison). No compiler warning from the P13 files (gcc, clang).
- **Coverage (gcc, gcov):** `TMCDebugBridge.cpp` 166 / 166 lines (100 %), 9 / 9 functions; driver tier 2542 / 2762 lines (92.0 %): `TMC4671.cpp` 1731 / 1928, `ODriveCAN.cpp` 263 / 269, `VescCAN.cpp` 382 / 399. `tools/coverage_report.py` now counts `TMCDebugBridge` in the driver tier (it was in the out-of-scope list).
- **Not verified:** the target (ARM) behaviour, the TMCL-IDE itself, a real board; the opcodes 143 / 146 / 148 against any specification.

### P16 T1 result (2026-11-10, approved, committed `70a7598b`)

- **Wiring:** `RMDCAN` on in `platform/target_f407_drivers/target_constants.h` (ffb_driver_tests only), `RmdMotorCAN.cpp` in `DRIVER_FIRMWARE_SOURCES`, `fakes/FakeRmdPeer.cpp` in `DRIVER_PLATFORM_SOURCES`, `test_driver_setup.cpp` now expects `RMDCAN` (MIDI still off). `RmdMotorCAN.h` needs no GCC extension: it builds with clang too (unlike P15), so there is no `HOSTTEST_NO_*` switch.
- **`fakes/FakeRmdPeer.{h,cpp}`:** one motor per node id on the fake CAN (SP 1.1.2 ids `0x140+ID` / reply `0x240+ID`, broadcast 0x280), commands 0x80/0x81/0x77/0x78 (reply = sent frame), 0xA1, 0x92, 0x94, 0x9A, 0xB5, 0xB6 (cyclic replies, period `(interval+1)*10` ms); strict violations (extended id, RTR, DLC != 8, unexpected command byte, non-zero NULL field, bad 0xB6 command or enable); fault injection (silent, delay, wrong node, wrong command byte, short DLC, raw inject, unsolicited reply); fidelity header written.
- **`support/RmdFixture.h`:** lead decision L4 settled: the delay budget is set to 1 ms so every `Delay` of `RmdMotorCAN::Run` parks the thread; the fixture measures the clock jump, puts the clock back and reschedules at the measured wake time (generic, handles the four different delays 100/20/150/1000 ms exactly). FIFO 1 modelling choice as in `OdriveFixture`. Fails on undeclared peer violations.
- **Skeleton:** `claude/cases/driver/test_rmd_setup.cpp` (7 cases: filters 0x241 / 0x242, 100 ms start, 0xB5 at 100 ms, 0xB6 at 120 ms and 0xB5 again at 1270 ms against a silent motor, motorReady against a replying motor for both instances, model command). The 1270 ms cycle is a `characterization:` case (constants not in the SP).
- **Gate (run by the lead):** `ffb_driver_tests` 1468 cases, 0 failed (default, DSP off), sanitizer 1453 run, clang 1245 (before: 1461 / 1446 / 1238; +7 skeleton cases); ctest 11 of 11 (DSP off 10 of 10); `check_baseline.py` OK (340 new cases, 0 failed); `flags.make` of all other executables identical to `p15t2-default`. No existing driver test changed (chooser lists with ids 11 and 12 did not break anything).
- **Not done:** all T2 tests (R1-R10), `COVERAGE_AND_SCOPE.md` (no coverage change yet), `RmdMotorCAN` in `coverage_report.py` `DRIVER_TRACK`. Nothing committed.

## P17 T1 result (2026-11-10)

- **Wiring:** `platform/usb_midi/target_constants.h` (F407 test header plus `MIDI`, first feature header in the include path of `ffb_usb_tests` only), `MidiMain.cpp` and `MidiHandler.cpp` in `USB_FIRMWARE_SOURCES`, `platform/src/host_midi_timer.cpp` (fake TIM9 = `TIM_USER`, initial state of `MX_TIM9_Init`) and `fakes/FakeTmcPhase.cpp` in `USB_PLATFORM_SOURCES`. `MIDI` is on nowhere else (`test_driver_setup.cpp:50` still passes).
- **Driver double (lead decisions L1, L7):** `MidiMain.h` includes `TMC4671.h`; the real header exists only with `TMC4671DRIVER` (off). `platform/usb_midi/TMC4671.h` is a recording double of the used interface (`FakeTmcPhase`: calls with arguments in order, configurable `initialize()`, motor type, `hasPower`, `isSetUp`; fidelity header written). **An include directory cannot shadow it:** a quoted include in `MidiMain.h` finds the real header in its own directory first, so the double has the include guard of the real header (`TMC4671_H_`) and is force-included (`-include`, C++ sources of `ffb_usb_tests` only).
- **Shim (L8):** `platform/usb_midi/cmsis_os2.h` provides `osDelay` (via the fake `vTaskDelay`, 1 kHz) for `MidiMain::update()`; the fake platform had no `cmsis_os2.h`.
- **Helpers:** `UsbHost::midiStreamingInterface()` (additive edit of a shared support header), `support/MidiFixture.h` (`MidiFixture`, `MidiProbe`, `MidiMainFixture`, packet builders).
- **Skeleton:** `claude/cases_usb/test_midi_setup.cpp`, 12 cases (all pass first run). Charter amendment: R6.2 treats `allowSlowSPI` as a field, not as a position in the call order.
- **Gate (run by the lead):** `ffb_usb_tests` 299 cases (287 + 12), 0 failed (default, sanitizer, clang 23, DSP off, coverage); ctest 11 of 11 default / sanitizer / clang / coverage, DSP off 10 of 10; `check_baseline.py` OK (340 new cases, 0 failed) in all five; `ffb_driver_tests` 1717 / 1701 (san) / 1494 (clang), unchanged; `flags.make` and object counts of `ffb_unit_tests`, `ffb_target_tests`, `ffb_flash_tests`, `ffb_rtos_tests`, `ffb_driver_tests` and `ffb_dsp_tests` identical to `p16t2-default`; no compiler warning from the new files.
- **Coverage (gcc, gcov, coverage build):** `midi_device.c` 131 / 204 lines (64.2 %, was 30 / 204) from the smoke cases; the ladder now counts `global_callbacks.cpp`'s MIDI lines (8011 of about 8099, 98.9 %). `MidiMain` and `MidiHandler` stay in `OUT_OF_SCOPE` of `coverage_report.py` until T2 (they are compiled now, but their tier is decided with the tests).
- **Not done at T1 (done in T2, see below):** all T2 tests (R1-R12), the work orders, the reviewers, `coverage_report.py` tier for `MidiMain` / `MidiHandler`, `COVERAGE_AND_SCOPE.md` numbers for the MIDI files. T1 is committed (`88980f93`).

## P18 result (32-bit build, T1-T4 approved, committed `544b4162` and pushed 2026-11-11)

- **Scope:** the whole suite built as ILP32 code (`-DFFB_TESTS_32BIT=ON`, `-m32 -msse2 -mfpmath=sse -funsigned-char`) so that `size_t`, `long` and pointers are 4 bytes as on the STM32F407. Plan and per-step results: `Tests/Code_Coverage/claude/plans/P18_32BIT_BUILD_PLAN.md`; work order: `Tests/Code_Coverage/claude/charters/P18_WORK_ORDERS.md`.
- **T1 (CMake):**
  - The option fails at configure time without 32-bit multilib, with MSVC or on macOS.
  - New ctest exclusion list `FFB_TARGET_TESTS_EXCLUDE_32BIT`.
  - 5 `__int128` DSP test files are left out (question 62 B); `test_dsp_filt_conv_q31.cpp` guards itself.
  - `run_all_tests.py` reads the 32-bit list.
  - Result: in the 32-bit build every executable gives the same case and assertion counts as the 64-bit build, except the 2 aborting cases of bug 225. 64-bit `ffb_dsp_tests` with the same 6 files excluded is 1871 cases, identical to the 32-bit build.
- **T2 (tests):**
  - `claude/cases_target/test_serial_ffb_32bit.cpp`, suite `SerialFFB32`, 31 cases: 18 `KNOWN_ISSUE_32BIT_UB`, 1 `KNOWN_ISSUE_32BIT`, 12 plain (2^32 addresses, 39 / 40 boundaries, positive controls).
  - Markers in `support/KnownIssue32.h`. `run_all_tests.py` classifies them by the build's width; tests in `claude/python/test_run_all_tests_ilp32.py` (10).
  - Writer and fresh reviewer: Sonnet 5.5. 42 mutants plus 5 lead re-checks; gaps F1, F2, F4, F5 and the survivor M31 were closed.
  - Accepted weakness: on ILP32 a mutant `setEffectState` guard `>` aborts inside the should_fail `state` case, which doctest counts as "failed as expected" before ending the run. `run_all_tests.py` shows it.
- **T3 (warning sweep):**
  - Method: all 586 firmware compile commands, recompiled for LP64 and ILP32 with `-Wsign-compare` and `-Wconversion -Wsign-conversion -Wshift-overflow=2 -Woverflow`, warnings diffed.
  - Only bug 225 changes behaviour. The rest are host artefacts (`size_t` narrowing that is a no-op on ILP32, integer-to-pointer casts of flash addresses, `%llx`) and an equivalent vendor shift (`arm_vlog_q31.c:118`).
  - Not covered by the method: unwarned `size_t` / `long` overflow, and struct layout.
- **New finding:** firmware bug 225.
- **Gate (2026-11-11):** ctest 11 of 11 in m32, default, sanitizer, clang and coverage; 10 of 10 DSP off; `check_baseline.py` OK in all six. Counts under "Current state". Coverage unchanged.
- **Not verified:** ARM itself. i386 differs in `uint32_t` (`unsigned int` versus `unsigned long` in newlib) and in the alignment of `int64_t` / `double` struct members (4 versus 8). Windows / macOS: the option refuses macOS; MinGW 32-bit is not tried.

## Suspected firmware bugs

| # | Module | Location | Description | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | EffectsCalculator | `FFBoard/Src/EffectsCalculator.cpp:1048` (`effectsForces`, `std::min<uint8_t>(cmd.adr, MAX_AXIS)`), read at line 1053 `effects_statslast[i].current[axis]` | Clamps the axis index to `MAX_AXIS` instead of `MAX_AXIS-1`, then reads `current[MAX_AXIS]` (`std::array<int16_t,2>`) out of bounds. Silent under old libstdc++, aborts under GCC 16 `_GLIBCXX_ASSERTIONS` at -O0 | Existing test `requesting the forces of a not existing axis is rejected` (`cases/test_effects_calculator.cpp:1062`, already `KNOWN_ISSUE_UB`). Message: `/usr/include/c++/16/array:210: ... std::array<short int, 2>::operator[] ... Assertion '__n < this->size()' failed.` -> SIGABRT after 192 of 317 cases. Excluded with `--test-case-exclude` |
| 2 | CMSIS-DSP (vendor, Tier C) reached from EffectsCalculator | `Libraries/CMSIS-DSP/Source/InterpolationFunctions/arm_spline_interp_f32.c:223`, called from `EffectsCalculator.cpp:695` with `blockSize = 1` | `while( *pXq <= x[i+1] && blkCnt > 0 )` dereferences `pXq` before checking `blkCnt`, so it reads one float past the 1-element input (`interp_time`, a stack variable). Bug is in the vendor library; the firmware call is valid per the API. On target it reads one adjacent stack word; the result is not used, so likely harmless | ASan stack-buffer-overflow (GCC and clang), test cases `reconstruction of a constant signal is constant` and `reconstruction of a step never leaves the range of the input values and reaches the target`. | 213 | RMD: maxtorque range | `RmdMotorCAN.cpp:102, 214, 350` | `maxtorque` accepts up to 65535 (65536 wraps to 0 with status OK) although the header says "max 0x7fff"; the flash word masks it (40000 gives 7232); `turn(0x7fff)` converts a float above 32767 to `int16_t` (undefined; the host gives -25536). Owner question 46 | KNOWN_ISSUE (B-RMD-3, 4 cases) + KNOWN_ISSUE_UB (1 case) |
| 214 | RMD: ErrorStatus bit field | `RmdMotorCAN.h:69-85` | The public `ErrorStatus` flags disagree with the SP 2.14 table from `overpower` on (field at bit 5 / 6 / 7 / 8 / 9 where SP has 0x0040 / 0x0080 / 0x0100 / 0x0800 and 0x1000 / 0x2000); one `overtemp` flag for two SP bits; no flag for 0x4000 (falls into `res2`). Only `asInt` is used: no behavioural effect today. Owner questions 48 and 52 | KNOWN_ISSUE (B-RMD-4, 6 cases) |
| 215 | RMD: position offset | `RmdMotorCAN.cpp:81, 93-95` | The offset (range +-35999) is saved as `uint16_t` and restored as `int16_t`: 35000 comes back -30536 (`getPos` 130536 instead of 65000), -35999 comes back +29537 (70463 instead of 135999). Owner question 47 | KNOWN_ISSUE (B-RMD-5, 2 cases) |
| 216 | RMD: interpolation dimension | `RmdMotorCAN.cpp:254-256` | In the request mode the 0xA1 handler adds `speed / (1e6 * dt_us)` (500 dps, 10 ms: 5e-11 degrees) instead of speed (dps) times dt (s) = 5 degrees; also the condition `ustime - lastTorqueStatusUpdate_us` is a truthiness test. Owner question 49 | KNOWN_ISSUE (B-RMD-6) |
| 217 | RMD: temperature type | `RmdMotorCAN.h:107`, `RmdMotorCAN.cpp:249, 288` | `curTemp` is `uint8_t`, SP 2.14 / 2.19 say int8: -10 degC reads 246. Owner question 48 | KNOWN_ISSUE (B-RMD-7) |
| 218 | RMD: command status | `RmdMotorCAN.cpp:350-352` | `maxtorque` discards the status of `handleGetSet`: an unsupported command type answers OK where every other RMD command answers ERR (theoretical, the parser's flag check normally rejects the type first). Owner question 51 | KNOWN_ISSUE (B-RMD-8) |
| 219 | MidiMain: note table | `MidiMain.cpp:107` (`noteToFreq[note->note]`), `MidiHandler.cpp:29-31` (no mask) | A note number with bit 7 set (malformed packet, e.g. `09 90 FF 7F`) is stored unmasked and used as an index into the 128 entry `noteToFreq`: out of bounds read. Intended: ignored (lead decision L13). Owner question 59 territory | KNOWN_ISSUE_UB (B-MIDI-1) |
| 220 | MidiMain: pitch bend | `MidiMain.cpp:148-155` | A pitch bend only changes the notes sounding at that moment; a note started afterwards plays unbent (a pitch bend is channel state, MIDI 1.0 spec). Owner question 55 | KNOWN_ISSUE (B-MIDI-2) |
| 221 | MidiHandler: `sysexState` | `MidiHandler.cpp:33-45` | `sysexState` is cleared only by a packet whose byte 1 is 0xF7; a SysEx that ends in byte 2 or 3 of the packet (`p6 02 F7 00`, `p7 02 03 F7`, `p6 F0 F7 00`, UM Table 4-2) leaves it true (not read anywhere today). Owner question 58 | KNOWN_ISSUE (B-MIDI-3) |
| 222 | MidiHandler: CIN ignored | `MidiHandler.cpp:24-31` | The code index number is never looked at: the single byte packets of an unparsed stream (CIN 0xF, UM Table 4-1 Note 2: `0F 90 00 00`) are decoded as events (a `noteOff(0, 0, 0)` for the status byte). Owner question 58 | KNOWN_ISSUE (B-MIDI-4) |
| 223 | MidiMain: `range` and `power` unchecked | `MidiMain.cpp:102, 116, 160-165` | Both commands accept any 32 bit value: `range` above 32767 makes `(int16_t)(sine * movementrange)` an out of range float to int conversion (undefined; x86 keeps the low 16 bits), `power` above 32767 reaches `setUdUq(int16_t, ...)` as a negative voltage (wrap-around, defined). Owner question 59 | KNOWN_ISSUE_UB + KNOWN_ISSUE (B-MIDI-5) |
| 224 | global_callbacks / MIDI class: no handler | `global_callbacks.cpp:355`, `midi_device.c:93-113` | While no `MidiHandler` exists `tud_midi_rx_cb` returns without reading: the first packet leaves 60 free RX FIFO bytes, `_prep_out_transaction` arms the OUT endpoint only with 64 free, so the endpoint NAKs for ever, also after a handler is created. Confirmed by experiment in T2. Owner question 60 | KNOWN_ISSUE (B-MIDI-6) |
| 225 | SerialFFB: negative effect address on 32 bit | `SerialFFB.cpp:157, 164, 179, 186, 193, 201, 208, 220, 227, 234` (`cmd.adr < effects.size()`), `CommandHandler.h:70` (`int64_t adr`) | The command address is `int64_t`, the guard compares it with `size_t`. On the 64-bit host (LP64) the comparison is unsigned and every negative address is rejected; on the 32-bit target (ILP32) `size_t` is converted to `int64_t`, the comparison is signed and any negative address passes: `effects[adr]` is read (`mag`, `state`, `type` get and every get) and written (`period`, `duration`, `deadzone`, `offset`, `sat`, `coeff`, `axisgain` set) outside the array; `state` set truncates to `uint8_t` first and answers OK instead of ERR. Reachable on shipped firmware with `FFBHIDEXT` (F407VG, F407VG_DISCO, F411RE): serial `fxm.0.period=5?-1`, HID command report with addr all ones, CAN. Found by the P18 32-bit build. Suggested fix: `cmd.adr >= 0 && cmd.adr < (int64_t)effects.size()` (a `(size_t)` cast is not a fix on ILP32: 2^32 becomes slot 0) | `test_serial_ffb_32bit.cpp`: 18 `KNOWN_ISSUE_32BIT_UB` (skipped on ILP32, abort with `--no-skip`), 1 `KNOWN_ISSUE_32BIT` (`state` set); the existing `mag` / `state` rejection cases are excluded in 32-bit builds (`FFB_TARGET_TESTS_EXCLUDE_32BIT`) |

## Untestable code and refactor suggestions

| Module | Location | Why untestable | Suggested refactor |
|---|---|---|---|
| CDCcomm | `CDCcomm.cpp:19-25` | Private constructor/destructor, never called | none needed (static class) |
| ErrorPrinter | `ErrorHandler.cpp:162-167` | Output branch needs a real `SystemCommands` (not compiled in `ffb_unit_tests`) | Covered in P4 (`ffb_target_tests`) |
| CAN/UART dispatch | `global_callbacks.cpp` | Not compiled in `ffb_unit_tests`; P1 tests mirror the loop in a local helper | Real file in P4 |
| RingBufferWrapper | copy constructor | Owns raw memory with an implicit copy (rule of three broken); works only by guaranteed elision (`CmdParser.cpp:20`, `CommandInterface.h:48`) | Delete copy, add move |
| Concurrency | all ISR/thread interaction | No preemption on the host | Keep ISR handlers small and testable as functions |
| CubeMX init | `Targets/*/Core/Src/*` | Hardware init only, not tested by decision | none |
| MidiMain | `UserExtensions/Src/MidiMain.cpp` | Out of scope. Constructs `TMC_1` directly (no `MotorDriver` interface use) | Take the driver from a chooser or as `MotorDriver*` |

## Host-vs-target caveats

| Module | Issue (size, packing, endianness, float/double, overflow, shift) | Test affected |
|---|---|---|
| all | Float -> int conversion of out-of-range values: ARM VCVT saturates, x86-64 SSE returns 0x80000000. Tests must not depend on out-of-range casts | Axis torque clipping, effect calculations |
| all | `long`/`size_t`/pointers are 64-bit on host, 32-bit on target. `int`/`int32_t` are 32-bit on both | `CommandHandler.cpp:136` `%lX`; `CommandReply::size()` uses `sizeof(CommandReply)` (contains `std::string`: 32 B host vs 24 B target), so reply memory accounting differs |
| HID reports | `__attribute__((packed))` structs in `ffb_defs.h`, `HidCommandInterface.h`: same layout with GCC on little-endian Linux; MinGW needs `-mno-ms-bitfields` (already set) | HID report tests |
| all | Implicit `double` promotion (e.g. `INTERNAL_AXIS_DAMPER_SCALER 0.7`, unsuffixed literals): target computes doubles in software; IEEE results are the same on host (SSE, no x87), so values match but timing does not | Axis/effects numeric tests: compare with tolerances |
| DSP | `USE_DSP_FUNCTIONS` uses the portable C CMSIS-DSP on the host, not the Cortex-M4 optimised paths. Small float differences possible | Filters, effects, Tier C: use tolerances, run both DSP ON and OFF |
| Endianness | Both little-endian. `memcpy` of CAN payloads (`CanCommandInterface.cpp`) behaves the same | CAN tests |
| Signed shifts | Arithmetic right shift on both (GCC/clang) | Encoder decoding |
| Filters | Host CMSIS-DSP sin/cos are the portable C versions without FMA; the Fc = 0.5 KNOWN_ISSUE depends on `arm_cos_f32` returning exactly 0 | `test_filters.cpp` (tolerances: relative 2e-4) |
| RTOS wrappers | Exception paths (`throw` on create failure) exist only on the host; the target defines `CPP_FREERTOS_NO_EXCEPTIONS` | `test_rtos_wrappers.cpp` (paths not tested) |
| CDC command interface | `CommandResult::size()` uses `sizeof` (contains `std::string`), so buffer thresholds differ host/target | `test_command_handler_gaps.cpp` CDC cases rely only on "at least the buffer size" |
| Axis | `Axis.cpp:1026, 1347` call `abs()` on floats; the float overload comes only through a transitive `<math.h>` (`TMC4671.h -> SPI.h`). `ffb_target_tests` compiles `Axis.cpp` with `-include math.h` to match the target (was bug 34) | `test_axis_torque.cpp` expo cases |
| Axis | `getLastScaledEnc` out-of-range float->int: host INT_MIN, ARM saturates (bug 36) | `KNOWN_ISSUE_UB` case |
| flash | `ffb_flash_tests` maps memory at the target addresses (0x08000000, 0x1FFF0000); 32-bit addresses become pointers (host warnings). Fake flash is instantaneous (no BSY/timing); partial erase / torn programs are a deterministic model of an indeterminate real state. Linux only verified; Windows untested; not built on macOS (`__PAGEZERO`, decision 2026-10-24) | `ffb_flash_tests` |
| SystemCommands | `NVIC_SystemReset`/`RebootDFU` return on the host: `reboot`, `dfu` and a main class change finish with OK on the host (no reply on the target). `taskstats`/`tasklist`/`otp` are registered on the F407 but not compiled in `ffb_target_tests`. `uid` info `%08lx` with `uint32_t` (host type mismatch, like bug 3). **`mallinfo()` may be missing on MinGW: `ffb_target_tests` (compiles `SystemCommands.cpp` since P2) may not build on Windows - unverified** | `test_system_commands.cpp` |
| cppmain | On the target `mainclass` is in NOLOAD `.ccmram` (`STM32F407VGTX_FLASH.ld:150`) and not zeroed before `cppmain` sets it; on the host it starts as nullptr (null checks such as `tud_cdc_rx_cb`'s only help after cppmain ran) | `test_global_callbacks.cpp` |
| portability | `ffb_target_tests` relies on weak-symbol overriding (test stand-ins for TinyUSB weak defaults, main.c's strong `HAL_TIM_PeriodElapsedCallback`) and on the MinGW `mallinfo` shim: both unverified on MinGW/PE; `host_cppmain.cpp` `#define`s `malloc`/`free` (formally reserved, works on GCC/Clang, guarded by a configure-time include check) | `ffb_target_tests` on Windows |
| voltagesense | `VREFINT_CAL_VREF` / `TEMPSENSOR_CAL_VREFANALOG` are `3300UL`: 64-bit on the LP64 host, 32-bit on ARM; `data * ADC_INTREF_VOL` overflows only on the target and only for a VREFINT reading below about 5 counts (not reached by tests) | - |
| inputs | CanButtons reads `msg.data` as `uint64_t` (4-byte aligned `CAN_rx_msg`: fine for LDRD on Cortex-M4, tests use `alignas(8)` for UBSan); 64-bit button states shared between ISR and thread can tear on the 32-bit target; shift by >= 64 differs x86/ARM (bug 73); bug 55 padding layout identical on host and target | P5 button tests |
| all target tests | `ffb_target_tests` uses `-funsigned-char` like ARM; `ffb_unit_tests` keeps signed `char` | char-dependent code |
| ringbuffer | `size_t` 64-bit host / 32-bit target with `uint32_t` temporaries; same results for tested sizes | `test_ringbuffer.cpp` |
| EncoderLocal | `x % 0` (bug 79): SIGFPE on x86, the Cortex-M4 UDIV gives 0 and `x % 0` yields the dividend (no DIV_0_TRP) | skipped case "setting the position after the index with 0 counts per turn is safe" |
| EncoderSSI / EncoderBissC | Shifts by >= 32 (bugs 84, 102, 103): x86 masks the shift count, ARM LSL by register >= 32 gives 0; `EncoderBissC.cpp:188` `(1 << (bits+2)) - 1` for >= 30 bits gives mask 0 on x86, -1 on ARM | bits range cases stop at 28/30 bits |
| EncoderBissC | `__builtin_clzll(0)` (bug 83) is UB: the host result (and very likely ARM, CLZ gives 64) still yields a CRC error; `optimize("-Ofast")` on `updateFrame` is honoured by GCC, ignored by clang (no floats) | KNOWN_ISSUE_UB_HIDDEN case |
| EncoderLocal | The fake raises the TIM3 update interrupt with zero latency and the index pin still reads low in the EXTI callback; on hardware the counter moves on before the handler and a narrow index pulse at high speed can end first | index and overflow cases |
| Encoders (threads) | No simulated time passes in a finite `Take` of a resumable task: a timeout is a second activation (models a DMA that never completes, not a timing claim) | "a waiting position request returns the last position when no frame arrives in time" |
| CanBridge | `uint8_t` positions in `cdcRcv` (`CanBridge.cpp:156`, `:165-178`): fine with the 64-byte FS `cdcbuf` of the F407; with a 512-byte HS buffer `while(pos < *Len)` never ends for `*Len > 255` (hang) | file header `test_canbridge.cpp` |
| CAN fake | Frames are acknowledged at once (`canSetTxAutoComplete`) and a mailbox is aborted at once even mid-transmission; on the target more than 3 frames per burst fail (bug 110) and an aborted mailbox finishes its current attempt. RDHR bytes beyond the DLC are not guaranteed on the target: tests that expose all 8 data bytes use 8-byte frames | CanBridge, CANInputMain, CANPort_2B cases |
| CANInputMain | `digitalBuffer` (64 bit) shared between FFBMAIN and the command thread can tear on the 32-bit target (NR 139) | none |
| SerialFFB | `% 0` (bug 125): SIGFPE on the host, `x % 0 = x` on the Cortex-M4 then infinite slope and saturating VCVT (likely full-scale torque); null Biquad (bug 124): SIGSEGV on the host, vector-table read and ~2^32-stage loop on the target | skipped crash cases |
| SerialFFB | `int64_t` address compared with `size_t` (bug 225): unsigned on the LP64 host (negative rejected), signed on the ILP32 target (negative accepted, out of bounds) | `test_serial_ffb_32bit.cpp` (`KNOWN_ISSUE_32BIT(_UB)`), 2 cases excluded in 32-bit builds |
| all (P18 32-bit build) | `-DFFB_TESTS_32BIT=ON` gives ILP32 like the target (`size_t`, `long`, pointers 4 bytes; SSE single precision; unsigned `char`). Not modelled by i386: `uint32_t` / `int32_t` are `unsigned int` / `int` (ARM newlib: `unsigned long` / `long`: overloads and `printf` formats differ, the i386 `-Wformat` warnings are not target bugs); `int64_t` / `double` struct members are 4-byte aligned (ARM AAPCS: 8), so struct layouts are not proven; double is hardware on the host, software on the target | 32-bit variant; struct layout needs the separate ARM compile check (proposal, not started) |

## Verification results (Phase 4)

Final gate after P9 (2026-11-02), run by the main agent:
- Full suite, all executables, 6 build dirs (GCC 16 / clang 23, DSP on/off, ASan+UBSan) and the `host-sanitize` preset out of tree: ctest 7/7 everywhere; numbers in "P9 results". Exclusions: `requesting the forces of a not existing axis is rejected` in `ffb_unit_tests` everywhere; plus the two spline cases in DSP sanitizer builds; `command with an unknown id is rejected` stays skipped.
- ASan / UBSan findings (GCC and clang): none (UB cases are marked `KNOWN_ISSUE_UB` / `_UB_HIDDEN` and skipped there).
- Coverage: in-scope firmware 98.9 %; tier A 98.1 %; tier B 85.1 %; tier C 100 % (details per phase).
- Uncovered code explained: yes, in each phase's results.
- Mutation check: per group in each phase; overall well above the 80 % target, survivors explained.
- `git status --short Firmware` shows only `Firmware/Tests/`: yes.
- No build artefacts written under `Firmware/`: yes (no `__pycache__`, builds out of tree).
- Nothing pushed, no remote or git config changed, `.github/` untouched: yes (final review FR1 checked the whole history).
- README instructions verified: yes (FR1; README rewritten in the wrap-up and its commands re-run).

All open ones are also collected in `Tests/Code_Coverage/claude/reports/OPEN_QUESTIONS.md` (keep both in sync).

1. ~~Coverage goal~~: answered 2026-10-23, 85 % or more, hardware-only code ignored (see decisions log). Original question: Is about 70% of in-scope firmware lines the right target? Going higher means testing interrupt and hardware-only code through fakes, which costs more per line.
